Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `int_sop_2` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
- output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`ax`, `ay`, `bx`, `by`) at one clock edge and `result` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `result` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `chainin` is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches `result` 2 cycles after it is applied. `chainout` is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value `result` loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before `result` shows it.
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by `reset`; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
- tie-off: `chainin` = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's `chainout` or a constant may drive it); `mode_sigs` = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. `chainout` reaches only the next block's `chainin`, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified raygentop under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 4.84712 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 6 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 4.7578e+06 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 25664, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 5.91854e+11. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in matmult: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) straight into a flip-flop (a product register), no adder and no LUT; wire 1.42 ns into the multiply, 1.21 out. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in matmult, is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) straight into another product register; wire 1.31 ns into the multiply, 1.21 out, 4.766 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: bilinearintrp.r, bilinearintrp.g, bilinearintrp.b (three separate 3-product sums, one block each)
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 6
STAGE_NS: 4.84712
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r (sum r): clk=clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,rud} with wire [6:0] rud = ru - rw, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,rvd} with wire [6:0] rvd = rv - rw, chainin=37'b0, result=res_r[36:0], chainout unconnected; assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2, where rwl1 <= rw; rwl2 <= rwl1 on posedge clk.
  • block sop_g (sum g): same shape with ay={12'b0,(gu-gw)[6:0]}, by={12'b0,(gv-gw)[6:0]}, ax={10'b0,u}, bx={10'b0,v}; assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2.
  • block sop_b (sum b): same shape with ay={12'b0,(bu-bw)[6:0]}, by={12'b0,(bv-bw)[6:0]}, ax={10'b0,u}, bx={10'b0,v}; assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2.
  • u and v stay the existing combinational selectuv mux outputs (lines 2818-2858), unchanged.

PLAN:

What the region is. In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2860-2886) nine products are formed from registered operands at one edge and loaded into i1r..i3b at the next; the three sums are purely combinational and are truncated to 7 bits: assign r = (i1r + i2r + i3r) with r a wire[6:0], likewise g, b (lines 2884-2886). i1r..i3b and ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl are read nowhere else in the module, and r,g,b leave only as the module's 7-bit outputs (used at raygentop.v:2011, 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484). So every sum is exact modulo 2^7, and the block's modulo-2^37 result carries that low slice exactly.

The regrouping (why T2, and why it costs no extra tile). wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (line 2862) wraps at 8 bits, so wl ≡ -1 - ul - vl (mod 128). Therefore
r ≡ ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128),
and the same for g, b. Three products per colour become two products plus one 7-bit constant-free correction term, which is exactly one int_sop_2. The operand differences are taken modulo 2^7 (ru - rw in 7 bits); since 128 | 2^19, feeding the 7-bit difference zero-extended into ay/by leaves the product congruent mod 128, and the block's unsigned 37-bit sum (max ≈ 2^15 here, never near 2^37) preserves it. No assumption about the design's inputs is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equal, including when wl wraps.

Registers and reset. Before: 2 stages from operands to the sum's inputs — ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl load at edge k, i1r..i3b load the products at edge k+1, the sum is combinational after edge k+1. After: 2 stages — the block's input registers take u, ru-rw, v, rv-rw at edge k, result loads the two products and their sum at edge k+1, and the -rwl2 correction is combinational after edge k+1. The operand registers ul,vl,rul,rvl,... and the product registers i1r..i3b move into the block; wl and its 8-bit subtract chain disappear entirely. The only registers added are a 2-deep 7-bit delay of rw, gw, bw (rwl1/rwl2 etc.), needed because the correction term must be the sample from edge k while being consumed after edge k+1. Result timing is cycle-for-cycle identical to the original.
reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks: bilinearintrp has no reset port and none of its registers is ever cleared, so a constant 0 reproduces it; with no enable the block's unknown power-up state flushes through in 2 cycles exactly as the original module's uninitialised registers do, which is what the testbench's lead-in assumes. chainin is tied to 37'b0 (no chaining), mode_sigs to 12'b0, and chainout is left unconnected — no block feeds another, so nothing needs its dedicated connection. Both product ports are used on every block, so no product port is tied off.

Candidates left, and why. All nine matmult products (raygentop.v:335-343) are left. Their sum is not a sum of the products: each product is truncated on its own (am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15], lines 348-350) before being added, and the result is only 16 bits. The block's internal sum adds the full products, which differs from the sum of the separately truncated ones by the carry out of the discarded low 15 bits of each product and by each product's discarded bit 31; recovering that would need the low 15 bits of each 16x16 product recomputed in soft logic (a second, soft multiplier per product), which is far more area than it saves. Using a block as a plain single multiplier instead is also a loss: synthesis already packs these two to a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, while a block takes a tile alone, so nine products would go from 5 tiles to 9. Nothing in matmult is converted; its product-register stage, which is the design's slowest stage, is untouched.

Blocks, tiles, and area. 3 blocks are added, one per colour sum; each takes a DSP tile alone, and the three mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles that held the nine bilinear products are freed, so the design still uses 6 DSP tiles — the area win is pure soft logic. Leaving the module: the 9 product registers (trimmed to their used 7 bits), the 6 7-bit adders of the three sum trees, the ul,vl,wl and rul..bwl operand registers, and the 8-bit 255-u-v subtract chain — roughly 150 flip-flops and ~58 LUTs. Entering it: six 7-bit operand subtracts, three 7-bit output corrections (~63 LUTs) and 42 flip-flops. The cluster count for bilinearintrp is flip-flop-bound in the baseline and drops by roughly two thirds; wirelength falls with the removed nets (the wl chain, the two-level adder trees and the operand register ranks) while the DSP net count is unchanged.

Timing. No stage contains more than one block — the longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, at the block's internal 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the 4.84712 ns critical stage in matmult, which this rewrite does not touch; the delay therefore stays at the baseline and the gain is area x wirelength. The two stages the rewrite lengthens or shortens:

  • Into the block (ay/by path): source register -> 7-bit subtract -> block input register, about 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 1.314 + 0.019 ≈ 3.39 ns at the median samples (≈4.64 ns if every element is taken at the top of its sampled range), under 4.84712 either way. This is the one stage that gains soft logic: one adder level that the baseline did not have on the ru/rv/rw path. The ax/bx path is unchanged (the same selectuv mux, now ending at the block's input register instead of ul/vl).
  • Out of the block: result register -> one 7-bit subtract -> the temp_shadedata* mux -> the shadedataa/b/c registers, about 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 1.032 + 0.019 ≈ 3.10 ns. Soft logic leaves this stage: the baseline had two adder levels here (the 3-input sum tree), the rewrite leaves one. Neither stage is the slowest, and neither is made slower than the 4.84712 ns stage.

Edit, concretely. In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2794-2886): delete the declarations of ul,vl,wl,rul,rvl,rwl,gul,gvl,gwl,bul,bvl,bwl and i1b..i3r; keep the selectuv combinational block producing u and v as it is. Replace the always @(posedge clk) block with one that only delays the three w weights two stages (rwl1 <= rw; rwl2 <= rwl1; and the same for gw, bw). Add wire [6:0] rud = ru - rw; and the five like it, three wire [36:0] result nets, the three int_sop_2 instantiations with named port connections as in MAPPING, and replace lines 2884-2886 with assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2;, assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2;, assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2;. The module's name, port list and port widths stay exactly as they are, since the equivalence testbench instantiates it directly. Change nothing outside bilinearintrp.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if matmult truncated once after the sum (Cx <= (am11 + am12 + am13) >> 15) instead of truncating each product before adding (lines 348-350), its three products per row would map onto a two-block chainout/chainin chain with the sum and the output register inside the blocks; as written, the per-product truncation makes the block's sum the wrong value.

18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: bilinearintrp.r, bilinearintrp.g, bilinearintrp.b (three separate 3-product sums, one block each)
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 6
STAGE_NS: 4.84712
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r (sum r): clk=clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,rud} with wire [6:0] rud = ru - rw, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,rvd} with wire [6:0] rvd = rv - rw, chainin=37'b0, result=res_r[36:0], chainout unconnected; assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2, where rwl1 <= rw; rwl2 <= rwl1 on posedge clk.
  • block sop_g (sum g): same shape with ay={12'b0,(gu-gw)[6:0]}, by={12'b0,(gv-gw)[6:0]}, ax={10'b0,u}, bx={10'b0,v}; assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2.
  • block sop_b (sum b): same shape with ay={12'b0,(bu-bw)[6:0]}, by={12'b0,(bv-bw)[6:0]}, ax={10'b0,u}, bx={10'b0,v}; assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2.
  • u and v stay the existing combinational selectuv mux outputs (lines 2818-2858), unchanged.

PLAN:

What the region is. In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2860-2886) nine products are formed from registered operands at one edge and loaded into i1r..i3b at the next; the three sums are purely combinational and are truncated to 7 bits: assign r = (i1r + i2r + i3r) with r a wire[6:0], likewise g, b (lines 2884-2886). i1r..i3b and ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl are read nowhere else in the module, and r,g,b leave only as the module's 7-bit outputs (used at raygentop.v:2011, 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484). So every sum is exact modulo 2^7, and the block's modulo-2^37 result carries that low slice exactly.

The regrouping (why T2, and why it costs no extra tile). wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (line 2862) wraps at 8 bits, so wl ≡ -1 - ul - vl (mod 128). Therefore
r ≡ ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128),
and the same for g, b. Three products per colour become two products plus one 7-bit constant-free correction term, which is exactly one int_sop_2. The operand differences are taken modulo 2^7 (ru - rw in 7 bits); since 128 | 2^19, feeding the 7-bit difference zero-extended into ay/by leaves the product congruent mod 128, and the block's unsigned 37-bit sum (max ≈ 2^15 here, never near 2^37) preserves it. No assumption about the design's inputs is needed; the rewrite is unconditionally equal, including when wl wraps.

Registers and reset. Before: 2 stages from operands to the sum's inputs — ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl load at edge k, i1r..i3b load the products at edge k+1, the sum is combinational after edge k+1. After: 2 stages — the block's input registers take u, ru-rw, v, rv-rw at edge k, result loads the two products and their sum at edge k+1, and the -rwl2 correction is combinational after edge k+1. The operand registers ul,vl,rul,rvl,... and the product registers i1r..i3b move into the block; wl and its 8-bit subtract chain disappear entirely. The only registers added are a 2-deep 7-bit delay of rw, gw, bw (rwl1/rwl2 etc.), needed because the correction term must be the sample from edge k while being consumed after edge k+1. Result timing is cycle-for-cycle identical to the original.
reset is tied to 1'b0 on all three blocks: bilinearintrp has no reset port and none of its registers is ever cleared, so a constant 0 reproduces it; with no enable the block's unknown power-up state flushes through in 2 cycles exactly as the original module's uninitialised registers do, which is what the testbench's lead-in assumes. chainin is tied to 37'b0 (no chaining), mode_sigs to 12'b0, and chainout is left unconnected — no block feeds another, so nothing needs its dedicated connection. Both product ports are used on every block, so no product port is tied off.

Candidates left, and why. All nine matmult products (raygentop.v:335-343) are left. Their sum is not a sum of the products: each product is truncated on its own (am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15], lines 348-350) before being added, and the result is only 16 bits. The block's internal sum adds the full products, which differs from the sum of the separately truncated ones by the carry out of the discarded low 15 bits of each product and by each product's discarded bit 31; recovering that would need the low 15 bits of each 16x16 product recomputed in soft logic (a second, soft multiplier per product), which is far more area than it saves. Using a block as a plain single multiplier instead is also a loss: synthesis already packs these two to a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, while a block takes a tile alone, so nine products would go from 5 tiles to 9. Nothing in matmult is converted; its product-register stage, which is the design's slowest stage, is untouched.

Blocks, tiles, and area. 3 blocks are added, one per colour sum; each takes a DSP tile alone, and the three mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles that held the nine bilinear products are freed, so the design still uses 6 DSP tiles — the area win is pure soft logic. Leaving the module: the 9 product registers (trimmed to their used 7 bits), the 6 7-bit adders of the three sum trees, the ul,vl,wl and rul..bwl operand registers, and the 8-bit 255-u-v subtract chain — roughly 150 flip-flops and ~58 LUTs. Entering it: six 7-bit operand subtracts, three 7-bit output corrections (~63 LUTs) and 42 flip-flops. The cluster count for bilinearintrp is flip-flop-bound in the baseline and drops by roughly two thirds; wirelength falls with the removed nets (the wl chain, the two-level adder trees and the operand register ranks) while the DSP net count is unchanged.

Timing. No stage contains more than one block — the longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, at the block's internal 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the 4.84712 ns critical stage in matmult, which this rewrite does not touch; the delay therefore stays at the baseline and the gain is area x wirelength. The two stages the rewrite lengthens or shortens:

  • Into the block (ay/by path): source register -> 7-bit subtract -> block input register, about 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 1.314 + 0.019 ≈ 3.39 ns at the median samples (≈4.64 ns if every element is taken at the top of its sampled range), under 4.84712 either way. This is the one stage that gains soft logic: one adder level that the baseline did not have on the ru/rv/rw path. The ax/bx path is unchanged (the same selectuv mux, now ending at the block's input register instead of ul/vl).
  • Out of the block: result register -> one 7-bit subtract -> the temp_shadedata* mux -> the shadedataa/b/c registers, about 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 1.032 + 0.019 ≈ 3.10 ns. Soft logic leaves this stage: the baseline had two adder levels here (the 3-input sum tree), the rewrite leaves one. Neither stage is the slowest, and neither is made slower than the 4.84712 ns stage.

Edit, concretely. In bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2794-2886): delete the declarations of ul,vl,wl,rul,rvl,rwl,gul,gvl,gwl,bul,bvl,bwl and i1b..i3r; keep the selectuv combinational block producing u and v as it is. Replace the always @(posedge clk) block with one that only delays the three w weights two stages (rwl1 <= rw; rwl2 <= rwl1; and the same for gw, bw). Add wire [6:0] rud = ru - rw; and the five like it, three wire [36:0] result nets, the three int_sop_2 instantiations with named port connections as in MAPPING, and replace lines 2884-2886 with assign r = res_r[6:0] - rwl2;, assign g = res_g[6:0] - gwl2;, assign b = res_b[6:0] - bwl2;. The module's name, port list and port widths stay exactly as they are, since the equivalence testbench instantiates it directly. Change nothing outside bilinearintrp.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if matmult truncated once after the sum (Cx <= (am11 + am12 + am13) >> 15) instead of truncating each product before adding (lines 348-350), its three products per row would map onto a two-block chainout/chainin chain with the sum and the output register inside the blocks; as written, the per-product truncation makes the block's sum the wrong value.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

REVISE

Required item mis-stated — "how many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses" (TILES).

  • The plan states TILES: 6 and "the design still uses 6 DSP tiles". This contradicts the plan's own accounting and the candidate table. The design contains exactly 18 multiplies (raygentop.v:335-343 and raygentop.v:2874-2882 — no others; grep of the whole file shows no further * operators). Per the candidate table, the nine bilinearintrp products sit on 3 tiles (mult_9x9_fixed_pt, nine products on three tiles) and the nine matmult products sit two to a tile in two_mult_18x19 — 5 tiles, the figure the plan itself uses ("nine products would go from 5 tiles to 9"). Baseline is therefore 3 + 5 = 8 tiles, and after the rewrite the three int_sop_2 blocks take a tile each while the three 9x9 tiles are freed: 3 + 5 = 8 tiles, unchanged. No reading of the table yields 6.
  • Change: state TILES: 8 (3 blocks for bilinearintrp + 5 unchanged matmult tiles), and correct the prose "still uses 6 DSP tiles" to the same figure; the "no net tile change / the win is pure soft logic" argument is unaffected.

Everything else checks out and needs no change:

  • Same behavior / Terms. wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (raygentop.v:2862) is an 8-bit assignment, so wl ≡ -1 - u - v (mod 128); r, g, b are 7-bit wires (raygentop.v:2786-2791) assigned the untruncated 15-bit product sums (raygentop.v:2884-2886), so each output is exact mod 128 and ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl. Feeding the 7-bit differences zero-extended into ay/by preserves the congruence (128 | 2^19), products reach at most 255*127 each and the 37-bit sum never wraps, so result[6:0] - rwl2 reproduces the original exactly. No ASSUMES: is needed and none is claimed.
  • Visibility. ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl and i1r..i3b (raygentop.v:2794-2816) are read only inside the always block and the three assigns; r,g,b leave only through the module ports, read at raygentop.v:2011 (linearmap takes blb,blg), 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484 — all of the plan's cited readers are correct.
  • Registers and reset. bilinearintrp has no reset and no enable on raygentop.v:2860-2883, so reset=1'b0 is faithful; with no enable the block's X power-up flushes in 2 cycles, and tb_equiv_bilin.v holds cmp=0 for an 8-cycle zero lead-in (lines 214-219), so nothing relies on an initial value. 2 operand-to-result stages before and after, with rwl1/rwl2 correctly supplying the sample-k weight after edge k+1.
  • Candidates left. The nine matmult products are correctly left: raygentop.v:348-350 truncate each 32-bit product to [30:15] before summing into 16-bit Cx/Cy/Cz (raygentop.v:313-319), which the block's full-width internal sum does not reproduce.
  • Ports: every input port connected by name, both product pairs used, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, chainout unread.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified raygentop under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 4.84712 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 6 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 4.7578e+06 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 25664, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 5.91854e+11. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in matmult: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) straight into a flip-flop (a product register), no adder and no LUT; wire 1.42 ns into the multiply, 1.21 out. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in matmult, is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) straight into another product register; wire 1.31 ns into the multiply, 1.21 out, 4.766 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

A row marked "on this design" is measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block; the other rows are sampled over earlier runs of other designs on this architecture.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

matmult.$mul$raygentop.v:342$22on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, two to a tile
matmult.$mul$raygentop.v:343$23on a DSP tile in two_mult_18x19 mode, two to a tile
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
The plan reviewer's reply to your previous plan

I'll read the design source now.

REVISE

Required item mis-stated — "how many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses" (TILES).

  • The plan states TILES: 6 and "the design still uses 6 DSP tiles". This contradicts the plan's own accounting and the candidate table. The design contains exactly 18 multiplies (raygentop.v:335-343 and raygentop.v:2874-2882 — no others; grep of the whole file shows no further * operators). Per the candidate table, the nine bilinearintrp products sit on 3 tiles (mult_9x9_fixed_pt, nine products on three tiles) and the nine matmult products sit two to a tile in two_mult_18x19 — 5 tiles, the figure the plan itself uses ("nine products would go from 5 tiles to 9"). Baseline is therefore 3 + 5 = 8 tiles, and after the rewrite the three int_sop_2 blocks take a tile each while the three 9x9 tiles are freed: 3 + 5 = 8 tiles, unchanged. No reading of the table yields 6.
  • Change: state TILES: 8 (3 blocks for bilinearintrp + 5 unchanged matmult tiles), and correct the prose "still uses 6 DSP tiles" to the same figure; the "no net tile change / the win is pure soft logic" argument is unaffected.

Everything else checks out and needs no change:

  • Same behavior / Terms. wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (raygentop.v:2862) is an 8-bit assignment, so wl ≡ -1 - u - v (mod 128); r, g, b are 7-bit wires (raygentop.v:2786-2791) assigned the untruncated 15-bit product sums (raygentop.v:2884-2886), so each output is exact mod 128 and ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl. Feeding the 7-bit differences zero-extended into ay/by preserves the congruence (128 | 2^19), products reach at most 255*127 each and the 37-bit sum never wraps, so result[6:0] - rwl2 reproduces the original exactly. No ASSUMES: is needed and none is claimed.
  • Visibility. ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl and i1r..i3b (raygentop.v:2794-2816) are read only inside the always block and the three assigns; r,g,b leave only through the module ports, read at raygentop.v:2011 (linearmap takes blb,blg), 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484 — all of the plan's cited readers are correct.
  • Registers and reset. bilinearintrp has no reset and no enable on raygentop.v:2860-2883, so reset=1'b0 is faithful; with no enable the block's X power-up flushes in 2 cycles, and tb_equiv_bilin.v holds cmp=0 for an 8-cycle zero lead-in (lines 214-219), so nothing relies on an initial value. 2 operand-to-result stages before and after, with rwl1/rwl2 correctly supplying the sample-k weight after edge k+1.
  • Candidates left. The nine matmult products are correctly left: raygentop.v:348-350 truncate each 32-bit product to [30:15] before summing into 16-bit Cx/Cy/Cz (raygentop.v:313-319), which the block's full-width internal sum does not reproduce.
  • Ports: every input port connected by name, both product pairs used, chainin=37'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, chainout unread.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three output sums of bilinearintrp — r (raygentop.v:2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 8
STAGE_NS: 4.84712
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r (output r): ax = {10'b0, u[7:0]} (the combinational mux output, raygentop.v:2818-2858), ay = {12'b0, dru} with dru = ru - rw (7-bit, wraps mod 128), bx = {10'b0, v[7:0]}, by = {12'b0, drv} with drv = rv - rw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_r[36:0], chainout unconnected. Outside: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rw2;
  • block sop_g (output g): ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, dgu} with dgu = gu - gw, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, dgv} with dgv = gv - gw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_g, chainout unconnected. Outside: assign g = res_g[6:0] - gw2;
  • block sop_b (output b): ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, dbu} with dbu = bu - bw, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, dbv} with dbv = bv - bw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_b, chainout unconnected. Outside: assign b = res_b[6:0] - bw2;
  • new 7-bit delay registers, clocked on clk: rw1 <= rw; rw2 <= rw1; gw1 <= gw; gw2 <= gw1; bw1 <= bw; bw2 <= bw1.

PLAN:

What to edit. Only module bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887). Nothing outside it changes: the instantiation at raygentop.v:2012 keeps its port list, and r,g,b keep their widths, meaning and cycle.

The regrouping (why T2). Each colour is a 3-product sum: r = (i1r + i2r + i3r) truncated to 7 bits (raygentop.v:2884, r is a 7-bit wire, raygentop.v:2786-2787), with i1r = ul*rul, i2r = vl*rvl, i3r = wl*rwl (2874-2876). The block holds only two products, so the three are regrouped into two. wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v is an 8-bit assignment (raygentop.v:2798, 2862), so wl ≡ -1 - ul - vl (mod 256) and therefore also mod 128. Since only 7 bits of the sum are kept:

ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

So one block per colour computes u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) and a 7-bit subtract outside the block applies the - rw term. The differences are formed 7-bit (mod 128) and zero-extended into ay/by; the block's unsigned arithmetic preserves the congruence because 128 divides 2^19 and 2^37. Operands reach at most 255 and 127, so each product is at most 32385 and the block's 37-bit sum never wraps; the result's low 7 bits are exactly what the original keeps. No ASSUMES: line is needed.

Edits in detail.

  • Delete the registers ul, vl, wl (raygentop.v:2796-2798), rul..bwl (2808-2816) and i1r..i3b (2799-2807), and the eighteen assignments that drive them in the always block at 2860-2883. Keep u, v (2794-2795) and the combinational case block at 2818-2858 unchanged — u and v now feed the blocks directly.
  • Add the six difference wires (dru, drv, dgu, dgv, dbu, dbv), each 7 bits, as plain subtracts of the module's 7-bit inputs.
  • Add the six 7-bit delay registers rw1/rw2, gw1/gw2, bw1/bw2 in a always @(posedge clk) block, as in MAPPING.
  • Add three 37-bit wires res_r, res_g, res_b and the three int_sop_2 instances, connected by name exactly as in MAPPING; every input port is connected, both product pairs are used on every block, chainin is the constant 37'b0, mode_sigs is 12'b0, and chainout is left unread (it has no connection to general routing).
  • Replace the three assigns at raygentop.v:2884-2886 with the three corrected ones in MAPPING.

Cycle alignment (register stages before and after). Before: operands (u,v and ru..bw) are captured at edge k into ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl; the products are captured at edge k+1 into i1r..i3b; the adder tree is combinational — 2 register stages from operands to output. After: the block's input registers capture u, v and the differences at edge k; result loads at edge k+1; the - rw2 correction is combinational — 2 register stages, the same. rw1 captures rw at edge k and rw2 copies it at edge k+1, so after edge k+1 the correction uses the weight from the same sample as the products. r,g,b therefore change on exactly the cycles they did before.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset or enable (raygentop.v:2762, 2860-2883), so the block's reset is tied to 1'b0, which is faithful: there is no reset behaviour in the original to reproduce. With no clock enable, the block's registers (and the new rw1/rw2 pairs), unknown at power-up, are overwritten unconditionally and flush in 2 clock edges; the reference testbench holds cmp=0 through an 8-cycle zero lead-in (sim_reference/tb_equiv_bilin.v:214-219) and runs no mid-run reset (MIDRUN_RESETS 0), so nothing relies on an initial value.

Visibility. ul,vl,wl, rul..bwl and i1r..i3b are read only inside the always block at 2860-2883 and the three assigns at 2884-2886, so all of them may move inside the blocks or disappear. r,g,b leave only through the module's ports as blr,blg,blb (raygentop.v:1990-1992, 2012) and are read at 2011 (linearmap takes blb,blg) and at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484 — all after the correction subtract, so all see the original values. u and v stay outside the blocks and keep feeding the mux logic as before.

Candidates left, and why. All nine matmult products (raygentop.v:335-343, ids $15..$23) are left. Their sums truncate each product before adding: Cx <= (am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15]) and likewise Cy, Cz (raygentop.v:348-350) into 16-bit registers (313-319). The block forms its sum from the full 37-bit products, and the sum of per-product truncations differs from the truncation of the sum by the discarded low-bit carries, so no in-block grouping reproduces Cx/Cy/Cz; recomputing the carries outside would need the full products back out of the block, which only result carries. These are also the design's two slowest stages, and a block there would add a register stage (2 cycles operand-to-result against the original multiply's 1), changing when Cx/Cy/Cz appear. So they stay as two_mult_18x19.

Blocks, tiles and area. The rewrite instantiates 3 blocks, one DSP tile each, and frees the three mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles that held the nine bilinear products, so the tile count does not change: 3 blocks + 5 unchanged matmult tiles = 8. (The per-candidate accounting in the table — nine 9x9 products on three tiles, nine 18x19 products two to a tile — gives 8 for the baseline as well; the run summary quotes 6 as packed at seed 1. Under either figure the rewrite's delta is zero, because three blocks replace three freed tiles.) The win is therefore entirely in soft logic and routing: out of the clusters go 24 flip-flops (ul,vl,wl), 63 flip-flops (rul..bwl), the 8-bit 255-u-v subtract chain, and the three truncated 3-input adder trees at 2884-2886; in come 42 flip-flops (the three rw1/rw2 pairs) and six 7-bit subtracts (three before the blocks, three after). Routing drops more sharply: the nine 15-bit product buses from the DSP tiles to the soft adder trees disappear, replaced by three 7-bit block results and three 7-bit corrections.

Stage delays. No stage contains more than one block — the three colour sums are independent outputs, so there is nothing to chain, and chainin is a constant on all three. The longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, at 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline critical path of 4.84712 ns.

  • Stage into the blocks (source register → mux or difference subtract → block input register): 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 1.314 + 0.019 ≈ 3.39 ns. This stage gains one soft level (the ru-rw subtract) over the baseline's register-to-register hop, and loses the 255-u-v subtract; it is not the slowest stage.
  • Stage out of the blocks (result → - rw2 → the registers that latch shadedata/linearmap): 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 0.621 + 0.019 ≈ 2.69 ns, shorter than the baseline's two-level adder tree in the same stage; not the slowest.
  • The slowest stage is untouched and remains matmult's hard multiply into its product register at 4.84712 ns, with the second at 4.766 ns. The rewrite is an area-and-wirelength win at unchanged delay, so the area x delay x wirelength product falls.

DESIGN_CHANGE: If matmult summed the full products and truncated once (Cx <= (am11 + am12 + am13) >> 15), its nine products would fit the block's full-width sums directly; that is a different computation from raygentop.v:348-350, so it cannot be done as a rewrite, but as a design change it would let the nine matmult products — including the two critical stages — move onto chained blocks.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.
