Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three output sums of bilinearintrp — r (raygentop.v:2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 8
STAGE_NS: 4.84712
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r (output r): ax = {10'b0, u[7:0]} (the combinational mux output, raygentop.v:2818-2858), ay = {12'b0, dru} with dru = ru - rw (7-bit, wraps mod 128), bx = {10'b0, v[7:0]}, by = {12'b0, drv} with drv = rv - rw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_r[36:0], chainout unconnected. Outside: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rw2;
  • block sop_g (output g): ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, dgu} with dgu = gu - gw, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, dgv} with dgv = gv - gw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_g, chainout unconnected. Outside: assign g = res_g[6:0] - gw2;
  • block sop_b (output b): ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, dbu} with dbu = bu - bw, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, dbv} with dbv = bv - bw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_b, chainout unconnected. Outside: assign b = res_b[6:0] - bw2;
  • new 7-bit delay registers, clocked on clk: rw1 <= rw; rw2 <= rw1; gw1 <= gw; gw2 <= gw1; bw1 <= bw; bw2 <= bw1.

PLAN:

What to edit. Only module bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887). Nothing outside it changes: the instantiation at raygentop.v:2012 keeps its port list, and r,g,b keep their widths, meaning and cycle.

The regrouping (why T2). Each colour is a 3-product sum: r = (i1r + i2r + i3r) truncated to 7 bits (raygentop.v:2884, r is a 7-bit wire, raygentop.v:2786-2787), with i1r = ul*rul, i2r = vl*rvl, i3r = wl*rwl (2874-2876). The block holds only two products, so the three are regrouped into two. wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v is an 8-bit assignment (raygentop.v:2798, 2862), so wl ≡ -1 - ul - vl (mod 256) and therefore also mod 128. Since only 7 bits of the sum are kept:

ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

So one block per colour computes u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) and a 7-bit subtract outside the block applies the - rw term. The differences are formed 7-bit (mod 128) and zero-extended into ay/by; the block's unsigned arithmetic preserves the congruence because 128 divides 2^19 and 2^37. Operands reach at most 255 and 127, so each product is at most 32385 and the block's 37-bit sum never wraps; the result's low 7 bits are exactly what the original keeps. No ASSUMES: line is needed.

Edits in detail.

  • Delete the registers ul, vl, wl (raygentop.v:2796-2798), rul..bwl (2808-2816) and i1r..i3b (2799-2807), and the eighteen assignments that drive them in the always block at 2860-2883. Keep u, v (2794-2795) and the combinational case block at 2818-2858 unchanged — u and v now feed the blocks directly.
  • Add the six difference wires (dru, drv, dgu, dgv, dbu, dbv), each 7 bits, as plain subtracts of the module's 7-bit inputs.
  • Add the six 7-bit delay registers rw1/rw2, gw1/gw2, bw1/bw2 in a always @(posedge clk) block, as in MAPPING.
  • Add three 37-bit wires res_r, res_g, res_b and the three int_sop_2 instances, connected by name exactly as in MAPPING; every input port is connected, both product pairs are used on every block, chainin is the constant 37'b0, mode_sigs is 12'b0, and chainout is left unread (it has no connection to general routing).
  • Replace the three assigns at raygentop.v:2884-2886 with the three corrected ones in MAPPING.

Cycle alignment (register stages before and after). Before: operands (u,v and ru..bw) are captured at edge k into ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl; the products are captured at edge k+1 into i1r..i3b; the adder tree is combinational — 2 register stages from operands to output. After: the block's input registers capture u, v and the differences at edge k; result loads at edge k+1; the - rw2 correction is combinational — 2 register stages, the same. rw1 captures rw at edge k and rw2 copies it at edge k+1, so after edge k+1 the correction uses the weight from the same sample as the products. r,g,b therefore change on exactly the cycles they did before.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset or enable (raygentop.v:2762, 2860-2883), so the block's reset is tied to 1'b0, which is faithful: there is no reset behaviour in the original to reproduce. With no clock enable, the block's registers (and the new rw1/rw2 pairs), unknown at power-up, are overwritten unconditionally and flush in 2 clock edges; the reference testbench holds cmp=0 through an 8-cycle zero lead-in (sim_reference/tb_equiv_bilin.v:214-219) and runs no mid-run reset (MIDRUN_RESETS 0), so nothing relies on an initial value.

Visibility. ul,vl,wl, rul..bwl and i1r..i3b are read only inside the always block at 2860-2883 and the three assigns at 2884-2886, so all of them may move inside the blocks or disappear. r,g,b leave only through the module's ports as blr,blg,blb (raygentop.v:1990-1992, 2012) and are read at 2011 (linearmap takes blb,blg) and at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484 — all after the correction subtract, so all see the original values. u and v stay outside the blocks and keep feeding the mux logic as before.

Candidates left, and why. All nine matmult products (raygentop.v:335-343, ids $15..$23) are left. Their sums truncate each product before adding: Cx <= (am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15]) and likewise Cy, Cz (raygentop.v:348-350) into 16-bit registers (313-319). The block forms its sum from the full 37-bit products, and the sum of per-product truncations differs from the truncation of the sum by the discarded low-bit carries, so no in-block grouping reproduces Cx/Cy/Cz; recomputing the carries outside would need the full products back out of the block, which only result carries. These are also the design's two slowest stages, and a block there would add a register stage (2 cycles operand-to-result against the original multiply's 1), changing when Cx/Cy/Cz appear. So they stay as two_mult_18x19.

Blocks, tiles and area. The rewrite instantiates 3 blocks, one DSP tile each, and frees the three mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles that held the nine bilinear products, so the tile count does not change: 3 blocks + 5 unchanged matmult tiles = 8. (The per-candidate accounting in the table — nine 9x9 products on three tiles, nine 18x19 products two to a tile — gives 8 for the baseline as well; the run summary quotes 6 as packed at seed 1. Under either figure the rewrite's delta is zero, because three blocks replace three freed tiles.) The win is therefore entirely in soft logic and routing: out of the clusters go 24 flip-flops (ul,vl,wl), 63 flip-flops (rul..bwl), the 8-bit 255-u-v subtract chain, and the three truncated 3-input adder trees at 2884-2886; in come 42 flip-flops (the three rw1/rw2 pairs) and six 7-bit subtracts (three before the blocks, three after). Routing drops more sharply: the nine 15-bit product buses from the DSP tiles to the soft adder trees disappear, replaced by three 7-bit block results and three 7-bit corrections.

Stage delays. No stage contains more than one block — the three colour sums are independent outputs, so there is nothing to chain, and chainin is a constant on all three. The longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, at 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline critical path of 4.84712 ns.

  • Stage into the blocks (source register → mux or difference subtract → block input register): 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 1.314 + 0.019 ≈ 3.39 ns. This stage gains one soft level (the ru-rw subtract) over the baseline's register-to-register hop, and loses the 255-u-v subtract; it is not the slowest stage.
  • Stage out of the blocks (result → - rw2 → the registers that latch shadedata/linearmap): 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 0.621 + 0.019 ≈ 2.69 ns, shorter than the baseline's two-level adder tree in the same stage; not the slowest.
  • The slowest stage is untouched and remains matmult's hard multiply into its product register at 4.84712 ns, with the second at 4.766 ns. The rewrite is an area-and-wirelength win at unchanged delay, so the area x delay x wirelength product falls.

DESIGN_CHANGE: If matmult summed the full products and truncated once (Cx <= (am11 + am12 + am13) >> 15), its nine products would fit the block's full-width sums directly; that is a different computation from raygentop.v:348-350, so it cannot be done as a rewrite, but as a design change it would let the nine matmult products — including the two critical stages — move onto chained blocks.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle. A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll read the design source.

APPROVE

Checked against raygentop.v:

  • Algebra. i1r+i2r+i3r is assigned to a 7-bit wire (2884-2886, r declared 7 bits at 2786-2787), so only the sum mod 128 survives; the 15-bit expression wrap is irrelevant since 128 | 2^15. wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (2798, 2862) gives wl ≡ -1-ul-vl (mod 128), so ul*rul+vl*rvl+wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl)+vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128). Operands ≤255 and ≤127 make each block product ≤32385, so the 37-bit sum never wraps and result[6:0] is the exact residue. Verified against the directed vectors: ovf-A (255,255,127/127/127) → plan gives 0-127 mod 128 = 1, original 64897→1; ovf-B → 43-90 mod 128 = 81, original 57425→81; noovf → 28 both ways.
  • Timing. Original: operands into ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl at edge k (2862-2873), products at edge k+1 (2874-2882), adder combinational — 2 stages. Block: inputs at k, result at k+1, - rw2 combinational — 2 stages, and rw1/rw2 delivers rw_k at edge k+1, so the correction is aligned with the products.
  • Visibility. ul,vl,wl, rul..bwl, i1r..i3b have no readers outside 2860-2886. blr/blg/blb are read only at 2011 and 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484 — all downstream of the correction subtract. u/v stay outside and keep driving the mux at 2818-2858.
  • Reset/power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset or enable (2762, 2860-2883); reset = 1'b0 is the only faithful drive, and int_sop_2.v:37-46 reloads unconditionally, so the block and the new rw1/rw2 flush in 2 edges inside the 8-cycle zero lead-in with cmp=0 (tb_equiv_bilin.v:214-219, MIDRUN_RESETS 0 at line 30-33).
  • Candidates left. matmult 335-343 truncates each product at 348-350 (am[30:15]) before summing into 16-bit Cx/Cy/Cz (313-319); the per-product truncation is not reproducible from the block's full-width sum, and the block would add a stage over the original 1-cycle am register. Single instance (177), so the tile accounting of 3 blocks + 5 unchanged tiles = 8 is consistent with the table; the 18 multiplies in the file are exactly the 18 candidates.
  • Required items. All nine converted regions and all nine left regions with reason, blocks (3), tiles (8), longest block series (1, 2.219 ns) against the slowest stage (4.84712 ns), soft logic leaving each stage with "not the slowest" for both, and before/after register stages (2/2) are all stated.

One non-blocking inaccuracy: the edit list calls the always block's assignments "eighteen"; it is 21 (12 operand copies plus 9 products), and the named registers make the deletion set unambiguous.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit raygentop.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356. The plan names their shared sum as 'the three output sums of bilinearintrp — r (raygentop.v:2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh raygentop.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically and no person reads this, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three output sums of bilinearintrp — r (raygentop.v:2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 8
STAGE_NS: 4.84712
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r (output r): ax = {10'b0, u[7:0]} (the combinational mux output, raygentop.v:2818-2858), ay = {12'b0, dru} with dru = ru - rw (7-bit, wraps mod 128), bx = {10'b0, v[7:0]}, by = {12'b0, drv} with drv = rv - rw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_r[36:0], chainout unconnected. Outside: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rw2;
  • block sop_g (output g): ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, dgu} with dgu = gu - gw, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, dgv} with dgv = gv - gw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_g, chainout unconnected. Outside: assign g = res_g[6:0] - gw2;
  • block sop_b (output b): ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, dbu} with dbu = bu - bw, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, dbv} with dbv = bv - bw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_b, chainout unconnected. Outside: assign b = res_b[6:0] - bw2;
  • new 7-bit delay registers, clocked on clk: rw1 <= rw; rw2 <= rw1; gw1 <= gw; gw2 <= gw1; bw1 <= bw; bw2 <= bw1.

PLAN:

What to edit. Only module bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887). Nothing outside it changes: the instantiation at raygentop.v:2012 keeps its port list, and r,g,b keep their widths, meaning and cycle.

The regrouping (why T2). Each colour is a 3-product sum: r = (i1r + i2r + i3r) truncated to 7 bits (raygentop.v:2884, r is a 7-bit wire, raygentop.v:2786-2787), with i1r = ul*rul, i2r = vl*rvl, i3r = wl*rwl (2874-2876). The block holds only two products, so the three are regrouped into two. wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v is an 8-bit assignment (raygentop.v:2798, 2862), so wl ≡ -1 - ul - vl (mod 256) and therefore also mod 128. Since only 7 bits of the sum are kept:

ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

So one block per colour computes u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) and a 7-bit subtract outside the block applies the - rw term. The differences are formed 7-bit (mod 128) and zero-extended into ay/by; the block's unsigned arithmetic preserves the congruence because 128 divides 2^19 and 2^37. Operands reach at most 255 and 127, so each product is at most 32385 and the block's 37-bit sum never wraps; the result's low 7 bits are exactly what the original keeps. No ASSUMES: line is needed.

Edits in detail.

  • Delete the registers ul, vl, wl (raygentop.v:2796-2798), rul..bwl (2808-2816) and i1r..i3b (2799-2807), and the eighteen assignments that drive them in the always block at 2860-2883. Keep u, v (2794-2795) and the combinational case block at 2818-2858 unchanged — u and v now feed the blocks directly.
  • Add the six difference wires (dru, drv, dgu, dgv, dbu, dbv), each 7 bits, as plain subtracts of the module's 7-bit inputs.
  • Add the six 7-bit delay registers rw1/rw2, gw1/gw2, bw1/bw2 in a always @(posedge clk) block, as in MAPPING.
  • Add three 37-bit wires res_r, res_g, res_b and the three int_sop_2 instances, connected by name exactly as in MAPPING; every input port is connected, both product pairs are used on every block, chainin is the constant 37'b0, mode_sigs is 12'b0, and chainout is left unread (it has no connection to general routing).
  • Replace the three assigns at raygentop.v:2884-2886 with the three corrected ones in MAPPING.

Cycle alignment (register stages before and after). Before: operands (u,v and ru..bw) are captured at edge k into ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl; the products are captured at edge k+1 into i1r..i3b; the adder tree is combinational — 2 register stages from operands to output. After: the block's input registers capture u, v and the differences at edge k; result loads at edge k+1; the - rw2 correction is combinational — 2 register stages, the same. rw1 captures rw at edge k and rw2 copies it at edge k+1, so after edge k+1 the correction uses the weight from the same sample as the products. r,g,b therefore change on exactly the cycles they did before.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset or enable (raygentop.v:2762, 2860-2883), so the block's reset is tied to 1'b0, which is faithful: there is no reset behaviour in the original to reproduce. With no clock enable, the block's registers (and the new rw1/rw2 pairs), unknown at power-up, are overwritten unconditionally and flush in 2 clock edges; the reference testbench holds cmp=0 through an 8-cycle zero lead-in (sim_reference/tb_equiv_bilin.v:214-219) and runs no mid-run reset (MIDRUN_RESETS 0), so nothing relies on an initial value.

Visibility. ul,vl,wl, rul..bwl and i1r..i3b are read only inside the always block at 2860-2883 and the three assigns at 2884-2886, so all of them may move inside the blocks or disappear. r,g,b leave only through the module's ports as blr,blg,blb (raygentop.v:1990-1992, 2012) and are read at 2011 (linearmap takes blb,blg) and at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484 — all after the correction subtract, so all see the original values. u and v stay outside the blocks and keep feeding the mux logic as before.

Candidates left, and why. All nine matmult products (raygentop.v:335-343, ids $15..$23) are left. Their sums truncate each product before adding: Cx <= (am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15]) and likewise Cy, Cz (raygentop.v:348-350) into 16-bit registers (313-319). The block forms its sum from the full 37-bit products, and the sum of per-product truncations differs from the truncation of the sum by the discarded low-bit carries, so no in-block grouping reproduces Cx/Cy/Cz; recomputing the carries outside would need the full products back out of the block, which only result carries. These are also the design's two slowest stages, and a block there would add a register stage (2 cycles operand-to-result against the original multiply's 1), changing when Cx/Cy/Cz appear. So they stay as two_mult_18x19.

Blocks, tiles and area. The rewrite instantiates 3 blocks, one DSP tile each, and frees the three mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles that held the nine bilinear products, so the tile count does not change: 3 blocks + 5 unchanged matmult tiles = 8. (The per-candidate accounting in the table — nine 9x9 products on three tiles, nine 18x19 products two to a tile — gives 8 for the baseline as well; the run summary quotes 6 as packed at seed 1. Under either figure the rewrite's delta is zero, because three blocks replace three freed tiles.) The win is therefore entirely in soft logic and routing: out of the clusters go 24 flip-flops (ul,vl,wl), 63 flip-flops (rul..bwl), the 8-bit 255-u-v subtract chain, and the three truncated 3-input adder trees at 2884-2886; in come 42 flip-flops (the three rw1/rw2 pairs) and six 7-bit subtracts (three before the blocks, three after). Routing drops more sharply: the nine 15-bit product buses from the DSP tiles to the soft adder trees disappear, replaced by three 7-bit block results and three 7-bit corrections.

Stage delays. No stage contains more than one block — the three colour sums are independent outputs, so there is nothing to chain, and chainin is a constant on all three. The longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, at 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline critical path of 4.84712 ns.

  • Stage into the blocks (source register → mux or difference subtract → block input register): 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 1.314 + 0.019 ≈ 3.39 ns. This stage gains one soft level (the ru-rw subtract) over the baseline's register-to-register hop, and loses the 255-u-v subtract; it is not the slowest stage.
  • Stage out of the blocks (result → - rw2 → the registers that latch shadedata/linearmap): 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 0.621 + 0.019 ≈ 2.69 ns, shorter than the baseline's two-level adder tree in the same stage; not the slowest.
  • The slowest stage is untouched and remains matmult's hard multiply into its product register at 4.84712 ns, with the second at 4.766 ns. The rewrite is an area-and-wirelength win at unchanged delay, so the area x delay x wirelength product falls.

The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

What changed — all inside bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762)

The nine product regions, regrouped onto 3 blocks. The three colour sums each had three products but the block holds two, so each sum is regrouped using wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v (so wl ≡ -1-ul-vl mod 128) and the fact that only 7 bits of the sum survive:

ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

  • $mul$...:2874$348 (ul*rul) and $mul$...:2876$350 (wl*rwl, its ul*rwl share) → sop_r.ax*ay with ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dru}, dru = ru-rw.

The leftover - rwl / - gwl / - bwl term of each regrouping has no block port, so it is applied outside: the three assigns at the end are now assign r = res_r[6:0] - rw2; and likewise g, b. Operands reach 255 and 127, so each product is at most 32385 and the 37-bit sum never wraps; the low 7 bits are exactly what the original kept.

Shared reduction. The three soft adder trees (i1r+i2r+i3r etc., old lines 2884-2886) are gone; each sum is now the block's internal ax*ay + bx*by.

Registers. Added res_r/res_g/res_b (37-bit) and the six difference wires. u, v and the combinational mux are unchanged and now feed the blocks directly.

Block connections. Every input port connected by name; reset = 1'b0 (the module has no reset or enable, so there is no reset behaviour to reproduce), mode_sigs = 12'b0, chainin = 37'b0 (unchained), both product pairs used on every block, chainout left unconnected and unread. No register the rewrite adds is relied on at power-up: with no clock enable they are overwritten unconditionally and flush within 2 edges of the testbench's zero lead-in.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/int_sop_2.v

{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/int_sop_2.v"
}
output
1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule
51

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

The file raygentop.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.

The approved plan

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three output sums of bilinearintrp — r (raygentop.v:2884), g (2885), b (2886); one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
TILES: 8
STAGE_NS: 4.84712
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r (output r): ax = {10'b0, u[7:0]} (the combinational mux output, raygentop.v:2818-2858), ay = {12'b0, dru} with dru = ru - rw (7-bit, wraps mod 128), bx = {10'b0, v[7:0]}, by = {12'b0, drv} with drv = rv - rw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_r[36:0], chainout unconnected. Outside: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rw2;
  • block sop_g (output g): ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, dgu} with dgu = gu - gw, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, dgv} with dgv = gv - gw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_g, chainout unconnected. Outside: assign g = res_g[6:0] - gw2;
  • block sop_b (output b): ax = {10'b0, u}, ay = {12'b0, dbu} with dbu = bu - bw, bx = {10'b0, v}, by = {12'b0, dbv} with dbv = bv - bw, chainin = 37'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, reset = 1'b0, clk = clk, result = res_b, chainout unconnected. Outside: assign b = res_b[6:0] - bw2;
  • new 7-bit delay registers, clocked on clk: rw1 <= rw; rw2 <= rw1; gw1 <= gw; gw2 <= gw1; bw1 <= bw; bw2 <= bw1.

PLAN:

What to edit. Only module bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2887). Nothing outside it changes: the instantiation at raygentop.v:2012 keeps its port list, and r,g,b keep their widths, meaning and cycle.

The regrouping (why T2). Each colour is a 3-product sum: r = (i1r + i2r + i3r) truncated to 7 bits (raygentop.v:2884, r is a 7-bit wire, raygentop.v:2786-2787), with i1r = ul*rul, i2r = vl*rvl, i3r = wl*rwl (2874-2876). The block holds only two products, so the three are regrouped into two. wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v is an 8-bit assignment (raygentop.v:2798, 2862), so wl ≡ -1 - ul - vl (mod 256) and therefore also mod 128. Since only 7 bits of the sum are kept:

ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl ≡ ul*(rul-rwl) + vl*(rvl-rwl) - rwl (mod 128)

So one block per colour computes u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) and a 7-bit subtract outside the block applies the - rw term. The differences are formed 7-bit (mod 128) and zero-extended into ay/by; the block's unsigned arithmetic preserves the congruence because 128 divides 2^19 and 2^37. Operands reach at most 255 and 127, so each product is at most 32385 and the block's 37-bit sum never wraps; the result's low 7 bits are exactly what the original keeps. No ASSUMES: line is needed.

Edits in detail.

  • Delete the registers ul, vl, wl (raygentop.v:2796-2798), rul..bwl (2808-2816) and i1r..i3b (2799-2807), and the eighteen assignments that drive them in the always block at 2860-2883. Keep u, v (2794-2795) and the combinational case block at 2818-2858 unchanged — u and v now feed the blocks directly.
  • Add the six difference wires (dru, drv, dgu, dgv, dbu, dbv), each 7 bits, as plain subtracts of the module's 7-bit inputs.
  • Add the six 7-bit delay registers rw1/rw2, gw1/gw2, bw1/bw2 in a always @(posedge clk) block, as in MAPPING.
  • Add three 37-bit wires res_r, res_g, res_b and the three int_sop_2 instances, connected by name exactly as in MAPPING; every input port is connected, both product pairs are used on every block, chainin is the constant 37'b0, mode_sigs is 12'b0, and chainout is left unread (it has no connection to general routing).
  • Replace the three assigns at raygentop.v:2884-2886 with the three corrected ones in MAPPING.

Cycle alignment (register stages before and after). Before: operands (u,v and ru..bw) are captured at edge k into ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl; the products are captured at edge k+1 into i1r..i3b; the adder tree is combinational — 2 register stages from operands to output. After: the block's input registers capture u, v and the differences at edge k; result loads at edge k+1; the - rw2 correction is combinational — 2 register stages, the same. rw1 captures rw at edge k and rw2 copies it at edge k+1, so after edge k+1 the correction uses the weight from the same sample as the products. r,g,b therefore change on exactly the cycles they did before.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset or enable (raygentop.v:2762, 2860-2883), so the block's reset is tied to 1'b0, which is faithful: there is no reset behaviour in the original to reproduce. With no clock enable, the block's registers (and the new rw1/rw2 pairs), unknown at power-up, are overwritten unconditionally and flush in 2 clock edges; the reference testbench holds cmp=0 through an 8-cycle zero lead-in (sim_reference/tb_equiv_bilin.v:214-219) and runs no mid-run reset (MIDRUN_RESETS 0), so nothing relies on an initial value.

Visibility. ul,vl,wl, rul..bwl and i1r..i3b are read only inside the always block at 2860-2883 and the three assigns at 2884-2886, so all of them may move inside the blocks or disappear. r,g,b leave only through the module's ports as blr,blg,blb (raygentop.v:1990-1992, 2012) and are read at 2011 (linearmap takes blb,blg) and at 2265-2267, 2423-2425, 2482-2484 — all after the correction subtract, so all see the original values. u and v stay outside the blocks and keep feeding the mux logic as before.

Candidates left, and why. All nine matmult products (raygentop.v:335-343, ids $15..$23) are left. Their sums truncate each product before adding: Cx <= (am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15]) and likewise Cy, Cz (raygentop.v:348-350) into 16-bit registers (313-319). The block forms its sum from the full 37-bit products, and the sum of per-product truncations differs from the truncation of the sum by the discarded low-bit carries, so no in-block grouping reproduces Cx/Cy/Cz; recomputing the carries outside would need the full products back out of the block, which only result carries. These are also the design's two slowest stages, and a block there would add a register stage (2 cycles operand-to-result against the original multiply's 1), changing when Cx/Cy/Cz appear. So they stay as two_mult_18x19.

Blocks, tiles and area. The rewrite instantiates 3 blocks, one DSP tile each, and frees the three mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles that held the nine bilinear products, so the tile count does not change: 3 blocks + 5 unchanged matmult tiles = 8. (The per-candidate accounting in the table — nine 9x9 products on three tiles, nine 18x19 products two to a tile — gives 8 for the baseline as well; the run summary quotes 6 as packed at seed 1. Under either figure the rewrite's delta is zero, because three blocks replace three freed tiles.) The win is therefore entirely in soft logic and routing: out of the clusters go 24 flip-flops (ul,vl,wl), 63 flip-flops (rul..bwl), the 8-bit 255-u-v subtract chain, and the three truncated 3-input adder trees at 2884-2886; in come 42 flip-flops (the three rw1/rw2 pairs) and six 7-bit subtracts (three before the blocks, three after). Routing drops more sharply: the nine 15-bit product buses from the DSP tiles to the soft adder trees disappear, replaced by three 7-bit block results and three 7-bit corrections.

Stage delays. No stage contains more than one block — the three colour sums are independent outputs, so there is nothing to chain, and chainin is a constant on all three. The longest series of blocks in any stage is 1, at 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, well under the baseline critical path of 4.84712 ns.

  • Stage into the blocks (source register → mux or difference subtract → block input register): 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 1.314 + 0.019 ≈ 3.39 ns. This stage gains one soft level (the ru-rw subtract) over the baseline's register-to-register hop, and loses the 255-u-v subtract; it is not the slowest stage.
  • Stage out of the blocks (result → - rw2 → the registers that latch shadedata/linearmap): 0.060 + 0.96 + 1.032 + 0.621 + 0.019 ≈ 2.69 ns, shorter than the baseline's two-level adder tree in the same stage; not the slowest.
  • The slowest stage is untouched and remains matmult's hard multiply into its product register at 4.84712 ns, with the second at 4.766 ns. The rewrite is an area-and-wirelength win at unchanged delay, so the area x delay x wirelength product falls.

DESIGN_CHANGE: If matmult summed the full products and truncated once (Cx <= (am11 + am12 + am13) >> 15), its nine products would fit the block's full-width sums directly; that is a different computation from raygentop.v:348-350, so it cannot be done as a rewrite, but as a design change it would let the nine matmult products — including the two critical stages — move onto chained blocks.

  • Scope / regions — only bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762-2883) changed; sim_run.log:3 subtree check confirms the rest of the file is identical, and the instantiation at raygentop.v:2012 keeps its port list. The deleted ul,vl,wl, rul..bwl, i1r..i3b are read nowhere else; the ul/vl at raygentop.v:2706-2756 belong to the preceding module and are untouched.
  • Ports — all eight inputs connected by name on each of the three instances (clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0,u} 18b, ay={12'b0,d..} 19b, bx, by, chainin=37'b0); widths match int_sop_2.v:17-24. chainout is left unconnected and unread, as the contract requires.
  • Terms / arithmetic — wl <= 8'hFF - u - v (8-bit, so wl ≡ -1-u-v mod 256, hence mod 128), and r keeps only 7 bits, so u*ru + v*rv + wl*rw ≡ u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw) - rw (mod 128). dru = ru-rw is 7-bit, and u*dru ≡ u*(ru-rw) mod 128 since 128 | 2^19 and 2^37. Products reach at most 255·127, so neither the original 15-bit i* regs nor the block's 37-bit sum wraps; the third term is restored by - rw2. Every term is accounted for.
  • Registers / timing — original: operands at edge k, products at edge k+1, combinational adder tree; new: block input regs at edge k, result at edge k+1, combinational - rw2. rw1/rw2 deliver the weight from the same sample k after edge k+1, so r,g,b change on the same cycles.
  • Reset / power-up — the original module has no reset, so reset=1'b0 reproduces it; the added registers are written unconditionally every edge and nothing depends on their initial value (x_on_dut_only: 0).
  • No ASSUMES: line is needed — the congruence holds for all input values.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

prompt

4 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: every register of the block is unknown until the first clear by reset; no register of the block has an initial value, and a rewrite may not rely on one.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified raygentop under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 4.84712 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 6 DSP tiles (as packed at seed 1) of the device's 132. Its used logic-block area is 4.7578e+06 minimum-width transistor areas (one DSP tile 253779, one cluster 27905) and its routed wirelength is 25664, so its area x delay x wirelength product, the objective, is 5.91854e+11. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in matmult: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) straight into a flip-flop (a product register), no adder and no LUT; wire 1.42 ns into the multiply, 1.21 out. The next slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report), in matmult, is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile (two_mult_18x19) straight into another product register; wire 1.31 ns into the multiply, 1.21 out, 4.766 ns, so shortening the slowest stage past that point buys nothing on its own. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original under it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when soft adders and flip-flops move into blocks.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone. Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
Objective

Minimize area × delay × wirelength against the baseline. If you expect no rewrite to lower it, output TIER: NONE. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: No rewrite: no candidate can be rewritten correctly.
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • A reviewer checks the plan automatically and no person reads it, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll read the source first.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
TILES: 6
STAGE_NS: 4.86883
MAPPING: no new mapping; the kept candidate (3 × int_sop_2 in bilinearintrp, ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,d?u}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,d?v}, chainin=37'b0, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, one block per colour sum r/g/b) stands unchanged.
PLAN:

No revision is proposed; the kept first-pass rewrite (bilinearintrp's nine products onto three blocks, product 0.820 of baseline) is the best available under the correctness rules, for the reasons below.

Candidates, converted or left:

  • bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348 … 2882$356 (all nine): already converted in the kept candidate, and the conversion is minimal. Each colour sum is u*ru + v*rv + w*rw with w = 8'hFF - u - v (raygentop.v:2862) and the sum truncated to 7 bits (raygentop.v:2884-2886), so it regroups exactly into two products, u*(ru-rw) + v*(rv-rw), plus a leftover 255*rw ≡ -rw (mod 128). Two products is exactly one block, so three blocks cover r, g and b; no alternative algebraic grouping removes the leftover linear term (eliminating u instead of w just swaps -rw for -ru), and no two of the six products can be packed into one multiply: packing u*dru and u*dgu into one ay needs 7 + 15 = 22 bits (21 even after reducing u mod 128, since the field must be 14 bits wide), against a 19-bit ay and an 18-bit ax. So 3 blocks is the floor, and dropping to 2 blocks by leaving one colour on a 9x9 tile keeps the tile count at 6 while removing less soft logic — strictly worse.
  • matmult.$mul$raygentop.v:335$15 … 343$23 (all nine): left, for one shared reason. The sums at raygentop.v:348-350 add the products after per-product truncation, am11[30:15] + am12[30:15] + am13[30:15], i.e. ⌊a/2^15⌋+⌊b/2^15⌋+⌊c/2^15⌋, whereas any block sums the full products and gives ⌊(a+b+c)/2^15⌋. The two differ by corr = ⌊((a mod 2^15)+(b mod 2^15)+(c mod 2^15))/2^15⌋ ∈ {0,1,2}, which is not recoverable from a block result: it needs the low 15 bits of each 16x16 product separately, i.e. three extra 15x15 low multiplies per sum. That is nine extra multiplies for the design, and no block mode produces the individual low parts, so no correct T1 or T2 rewrite exists. The only correct block form, one product per block with bx=by=0 and the original soft adders kept, needs nine blocks (nine tiles alone, +6 tiles ≈ +1.5e6 area, +32%) and turns the Cx/Cy/Cz registers into wires behind two soft adder levels, which both raises area and lengthens the stage feeding dir. A two-block chain per sum is also ruled out on timing: chainin is captured one edge after the first block's operands, so the chained result lands 3 cycles after the operands against the original 2, changing Cx/Cy/Cz by a cycle.

Blocks and tiles: the rewrite adds no blocks beyond the three already kept. The design uses 6 DSP tiles, the same as baseline (measured 6 at seed 1): the three blocks take the three tiles the nine mult_9x9_fixed_pt products used, so the conversion is tile-neutral and the 2.35% area drop is the soft logic it removes.

Longest series of blocks in any stage: one. chainin is tied to 37'b0 and chainout is left unconnected on all three blocks, so no stage contains two blocks in series. The block-internal stage is 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns, the stage into a block is 0.060 + wire (1.001 to 1.419 on this design) + 0.019 ≈ 1.08 to 1.50 ns, and the stage out of a block is 0.060 + wire + one 7-bit subtract + setup ≈ 1.7 ns. All are far below the slowest stage, 4.86883 ns.

The slowest stage, and why no candidate reaches it: at seed 1 the limiting path starts at a dffe (linearmap's ul/vl, raygentop.v:2706-2722), runs through the two 18-bit address adders of raygentop.v:2723-2758 ($add~377 1 carry stage, $add~380 16 carry stages, 2.61 ns cumulative), then three LUT levels of the 8-way factor mux (1.65 ns), into a registered hard-block input. That path is entirely inside linearmap's address arithmetic, which is not a candidate region and contains no multiply; the baseline's two reported stages (4.84712 and 4.766 ns, both a DSP multiply straight into a product register in matmult) sit level with it. So the design's period is floor-limited by at least two near-equal stages of different kinds, and even a correct matmult conversion (which does not exist) would leave this 4.87 ns path untouched. The measured +0.45% on period is placement noise against a baseline whose seed spread is about 4.3% on the product.

Soft logic that leaves which stage (kept candidate, for the record): the ul/vl/wl and rul…bwl operand registers and the nine 15-bit product registers i1r…i3b (raygentop.v:2796-2816, 2862-2882) leave the two register stages of bilinearintrp, and the six 15-bit adders of raygentop.v:2884-2886 leave the stage after the products; none of those stages was the slowest, which is why the gain shows as area (−2.35%) and wirelength (−16.44%), not period. What remains in soft logic is six 7-bit subtracts ru-rw etc. ahead of the blocks (in the stage that ends at the block input register, 1.08 to 1.50 ns), three 7-bit subtracts after result (in the stage that starts at the block output register), and the rw1/rw2, gw1/gw2, bw1/bw2 delay registers.

Register stages, before and after, for the converted regions: before, operands are registered once (ul, vl, wl, rul…bwl at edge k), the products are registered at edge k+1 (i1r…i3b), and the sum is combinational, so r/g/b reflect edge-k operands after edge k+1 — two register stages. After, the block registers ax/ay/bx/by at edge k and result at edge k+1, with the two products and their sum between them, and the leftover -rw is subtracted combinationally from result[6:0] using rw delayed two edges — two register stages, the same values on the same cycles. reset is tied low because the original pipeline registers have no reset in raygentop.v:2860-2883 and the blocks' results are only ever read through the same combinational tail; power-up is unconstrained for these registers in the original as well.

DESIGN_CHANGE: in matmult, truncating once after the sum (Cx <= (am11 + am12 + am13) >> 15 at raygentop.v:348-350, and likewise for Cy/Cz) instead of truncating each product first would make all nine matmult products convertible: each sum would become two blocks' worth of full-width products with no carry correction, and with the 3-product sums restructured to 2 products plus a chained block the matmult product registers and 16-bit soft adders could move inside the blocks. It changes the computed value by up to 2 LSBs per output, so it is a design change, not a rewrite, and it does not change the decision above.

+ int_sop_2 sop_r (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({10'b0, u}), .ay({12'b0, dru}),
+ .bx({10'b0, v}), .by({12'b0, drv}),
+ .chainin(37'b0), .result(res_r), .chainout());
+ int_sop_2 sop_g (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({10'b0, u}), .ay({12'b0, dgu}),
+ .bx({10'b0, v}), .by({12'b0, dgv}),
+ .chainin(37'b0), .result(res_g), .chainout());
+ int_sop_2 sop_b (.clk(clk), .reset(1'b0), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
+ .ax({10'b0, u}), .ay({12'b0, dbu}),
+ .bx({10'b0, v}), .by({12'b0, dbv}),
+ .chainin(37'b0), .result(res_b), .chainout());
+
+ assign r = res_r[6:0] - rw2 ;
+ assign g = res_g[6:0] - gw2 ;
+ assign b = res_b[6:0] - bw2 ;
endmodule

Objective loop

1 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 1
loop stopped by
planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 4.7578e+06 · critical path 4.84712 ns · wirelength 25664 · area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20) 5.91854e+11 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay x wirelength (delay register to register, 2026-10-20): VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded) times the critical path times the total routed wirelength. Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the product is the product of the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.32%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 4.32%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.
